Definitions

  • EPS is adjusted (recurring) EPS after about PLN 100m of minorities, on 2,243.7m shares. Reported EPS will be about PLN 0.10 to 0.27 lower.
  • Free cash flow is operating cash flow less capital spending less lease payments, before dividends and acquisitions.
  • Operating cash flow: PLN 10,860m, 10,430m, 10,900m, 9,620m and 9,760m. It is recurring EBITDA less restructuring and reclamation cash (PLN 500m to 600m), cash tax, cash interest (net financial items less about PLN 450m of non-cash unwinding) and the working-capital change.
  • Consensus comparison: FactSet FCF is minus 5,095 / minus 3,062 / plus 1,561 (FY2026-FY2028), on capital spending of 15,107 / 14,000 / 10,728.
Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: PGE Polska Grupa Energetyczna S.A. (PGE-PL, Warsaw; not Portland General Electric or PG&E): Poland's largest state-controlled integrated power group (State Treasury 60.86%); segments: Distribution (regulated grid), District Heating, Coal Energy (lignite and hard-coal generation plus own lignite mines), Gas-fired Generation, Renewables (onshore, solar, storage, and offshore wind including the 50% Baltica 2 joint operation with Ørsted), Supply (retail), and Railway Energy Services

Request: T-D1

Peers: TAURON Polska Energia (Poland's largest distributor, same regulator, coal fleet, rising leverage); Enea (integrated, coal mining, falling distribution return); Energa (Orlen subsidiary; distribution-heavy, delayed gas plants); Public Power Corporation (former state lignite utility that exited coal and grew regulated grids); CEZ (state-controlled integrated utility with coal and grid, state dividend policy); RWE (lignite run-down with state compensation, depreciation after write-downs); Ørsted (Baltica 2 partner, offshore execution record); ZE PAK (private Polish lignite generator closing its fleet) Operating conditions: the 2026 European gas-price shock and its normalisation; coal capacity-market support ending after 2028 under the EU derogation; Polish distribution tariffs set by the Energy Regulatory Office with a return tied to an 18-month average of 10-year government bond yields; below-cost regulated household tariffs; a general government deficit near 7% of GDP and the autumn 2027 parliamentary election; rising Polish bond yields; a corporate income tax rise from 19% to 22% from 1 January 2027 (passed by the Senate on 6 November 2026) Inputs: Can PGE grow distribution EBITDA in line with its regulated asset base (PLN 30.5bn in 2025, Bernstein) while the allowed return moves from the 12.96% earned in 2025 (Bernstein) under the 2026 WACC reduction seen at Tauron (-1.34 points) and the later rise in bond yields? Can PGE commission Baltica 2 (first offshore asset; first power targeted 2027, full operation end-2027) on time and budget, given its record on Dolna Odra and Gryfino CCGTs and Ørsted's record elsewhere? Can PGE run its coal fleet down after 2028 without state support, given that lignite costs are largely fixed (Bernstein, 8 May 2025), the PGG take-or-pay coal contract runs to 2028, and management plans Coal EBITDA of minus PLN 3.3bn in 2030 (Strategy 2035 update, 20 October 2026)? Can PGE fund PLN 117bn of capital spending to 2030 and a dividend of up to 5% yield from FY2026 earnings while keeping net debt below 3.5x EBITDA? Can district heating reach the PLN 3.4bn 2030 target given one-to-two-year tariff lags? What did the Efficiency Improvement Programme (35% lower forecast EBITDA losses in 2030) cover, and what have comparable programmes delivered? Focus: Which of PGE's expected changes rest on strengths it has demonstrated (regulated grid growth, contracted project delivery, capacity auctions) and which rest on strengths it would need to acquire (running coal for cash without support, cost elimination in lignite, pricing power in retail)? What do precedents say about how often state-controlled utilities with a coal fleet delivered their medium-term targets?

Request: T-D2

Peers: TAURON Polska Energia (same regulator and household tariff, earlier capacity-revenue cliff); Enea (coal mining, distribution WACC path); Energa (distribution-heavy); CEZ (coal run-down, windfall tax 2023-2025 and recovery); Public Power Corporation (lignite exit, adjusted-earnings definition); EVN and Verbund (Austrian windfall levy regime to 2030); RWE and LEAG (compensated lignite closures) Operating conditions: an energy-price shock followed by normalisation; the end of coal capacity payments in 2028; election-year price caps and windfall levies; rising sovereign bond yields; a corporate tax increase; capital spending above operating cash flow Inputs: How concentrated are the drivers that separate PGE's 2026-2030 financials from 2020-2025: coal capacity revenue after 2028, the distribution allowed return, Baltica 2 commissioning, the 2027 household tariff, windfall levies or energy-sector tax surcharges (Bill UD459, October 2026), depreciation from new assets, and interest on rising net debt? How large is the coal capacity revenue that ends after 2028? Bernstein reports PLN 2.25bn of conventional capacity revenue in 2024, while Fitch (28 September 2026) reports coal capacity revenue averaging PLN 914m a year in 2026-2027 and PLN 534m a year in 2028-2030; which perimeter does each cover? What is the historical dispersion of outcomes for Polish utilities' EBITDA and EPS against consensus 12 to 18 months ahead, and what events widened it (2016 coal-mine support, 2018 CO2 rise, 2022-2024 price caps and Price Difference Fund, 2025 NABE reversal and impairments)? How often were forecasts that rested on one or two policy drivers met? Focus: Over the next 18 months, how wide is the plausible range for PGE's EBITDA, adjusted EPS and free cash flow, and which one or two dated events (the December 2026 household and distribution tariff decisions, a levy, the post-2028 coal support decision) skew it?

Request: T-I1

Peers: TAURON Polska Energia; Enea; Energa (Orlen); CEZ; Public Power Corporation; Endesa (regulated networks under political price pressure) Operating conditions: preliminary quarterly results published about ten days before reports; no annual guidance, only 2030 targets; thin broker coverage (three to five brokers in FactSet); the 2026 energy shock; the corporate tax increase from 2027; the autumn 2027 election Inputs: What did PGE's Q3 2026 preliminary figures and report (24 November 2026) show against brokers' estimates, by segment? Have brokers (Trigon, Erste, PKO BP, BOS, Wood & Company, Noble, Bernstein, Santander, Pekao, IPOPEMA) incorporated the corporate income tax rise to 22% from 2027 and any energy-sector surcharge, the 2027 household tariff, and the end of the 2027 supplementary capacity contract? What FY2026-FY2028 EPS, EBITDA, capital spending and dividend do they now forecast, and how has each changed since October 2026 (FactSet median EPS at 1 November 2026: 2.90, 2.60, 2.47)? Who forecasts FY2029-FY2030, and do those forecasts include coal after 2028? How likely are the 2030 targets (EBITDA PLN 15bn) to be cut, met or raised, given PGE's record of revising targets? What dividend will be proposed in spring 2027, and how will the state vote? What do Tauron's and Enea's latest results and guidance imply for PGE? Focus: Over the next 18 months, are PGE's consensus EPS and free cash flow more likely to be revised up or down, and are the drivers EBITDA (spreads, distribution, coal) or items below EBITDA (tax, depreciation, interest)?

Request: T-I2

Peers: TAURON Polska Energia; Enea; Energa (Orlen); CEZ; Public Power Corporation; Endesa; Fortum (state-owned, power-price exposure) Operating conditions: a cohort of central and south-east European state utilities near the top of its valuation range after a 2026 rally; a risk-off market with rising bond yields; Polish fiscal stress; the 2027 election Inputs: How widely do sell-side analysts and investors (including on Twitter/X and Polish investor forums) discuss each of these for PGE? (1) The end of coal capacity payments after 2028 and the size of coal losses from 2029. (2) The distribution allowed return, and whether higher bond yields will lift it. (3) The corporate income tax rise to 22% and possible energy-sector surcharges or windfall levies. (4) A dividend paid while free cash flow is negative, and the state's vote. (5) Net debt rising towards the 3.5x ceiling, and the risk of a later equity raise. (6) Depreciation and interest rising as Baltica 2, Rybnik CCGT and grid assets enter service, and the effect on EPS against EBITDA (FactSet consensus at 1 November 2026: EBITDA PLN 13.37bn, 13.34bn and 14.05bn and EPS PLN 2.90, 2.60 and 2.47 for FY2026-FY2028). (7) The 2027 household tariff and election-year price caps. (8) Recurring "one-off" provisions and Baltica 2 derivative revaluations in reported earnings. (9) A post-2028 coal solution (strategic reserve, compensation, carve-out). Which analysts lead each theme, since when, and with what view? Focus: Which of these dynamics are widely priced in the narrative on PGE, which are discussed by only one or two analysts, and which by no one, especially the relationship between EBITDA and EPS over 2026-2030, and the path of leverage and the dividend after the coal capacity contracts end in 2028?

Request: OWN-1

Question: For PGE Polska Grupa Energetyczna (PGE-PL), what capacity-market revenue does the Coal Energy segment receive in each year from 2026 to 2030 under its existing contracts (the 2024 five-year contracts and the 2027 supplementary auction award of 1,859 MW)? How do Bernstein's figure (PLN 2.25bn of conventional capacity revenue in 2024) and Fitch's (coal capacity revenue averaging PLN 914m a year in 2026-2027 and PLN 534m a year in 2028-2030, 28 September 2026) reconcile? What is the status of Poland's negotiations with the European Commission on support for coal units above 550 g CO2/kWh after 2028, the strategic-reserve option, and statutory compensation for units kept open in the public interest?

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 15 November 2026, 13:59 to 14:21 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
PGE Polska Grupa Energetyczna (PGE-PL): answers to the third and fourth sets of questions

Written 13 November 2026 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Money in PLN; "bn" is billion, "m" is million. Fiscal year = calendar year.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Preface: bases, what I change, and what to treat with care

Bases used throughout

  • EBITDA is the company's recurring EBITDA, the basis of the second set and the one closest to the brokers'.
  • EPS is adjusted EPS after about PLN 100m of minorities, on 2,243.7m shares. The Financial Analyst's model does not deduct minorities, so its EPS is about PLN 0.045 higher in every year. Where I quote the model's own figure I write "model basis".
  • "The brokers" means the FactSet median, which rests on three or four brokers for FY2026 to FY2028 and on one for FY2029.
  • Prices: PLN 15.755 (6 November 2026, dashboard); the model uses PLN 16.30 (1 November 2026).
  • No third-quarter figures were available. The AlphaSense reports are dated 11 November (one 13 November). PGE's preliminary third-quarter figures had not been published; the report is due on 24 November 2026.

The one forecast input I change: the EBITDA margin (reason in D-01, point 1)

Distribution EBITDA in the second set is too high. I lower it, and nothing else.

PLN bn unless stated FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030
Distribution EBITDA: second set 5.70 5.80 6.05 6.30 6.70
Distribution EBITDA: mine 5.50 5.60 5.75 5.90 6.20
Distribution EBITDA: brokers' mean 5.33 5.17 5.27 n/a n/a (Bernstein 6.22)
Group EBITDA: second set 13.75 13.60 14.25 12.65 13.27
Group EBITDA: mine 13.57 13.37 13.97 12.25 12.77
Group EBITDA: brokers 13.37 13.34 14.05 14.09 (one broker) n/a
EBITDA margin input: second set / mine (%) 21.0 / 20.7 20.1 / 19.8 21.2 / 20.8 19.4 / 18.8 20.1 / 19.3
Adjusted EPS: second set (PLN) 2.82 2.43 2.26 1.50 1.47
Adjusted EPS: mine (PLN) 2.76 2.35 2.17 1.37 1.31
Adjusted EPS, model basis: mine (PLN) 2.80 2.40 2.22 1.41 1.35
Adjusted EPS: brokers (PLN) 2.90 2.60 2.47 2.39 (one broker) n/a

All other inputs stand: depreciation, net financial items, tax rate, dividend, capital spending, working capital, leases, acquisitions and share count. My EPS figures are my own arithmetic from the changed margins; the Financial Analyst's updated model gives the figures of record.

The gaps between the second set's cross-check and the model (the model summary's Part 4 table)

None of the gaps shows a wrong input, and none is material. Each has a mechanical cause.

  • EPS: the model is PLN 0.03 to 0.06 higher in every year. About PLN 0.045 is the minorities (PLN 100m over 2,243.7m shares), which the model does not carry. The rest is the rounding of the margin input to one decimal place. In FY2030, for example, 20.1% of revenue is PLN 13,294m against the second set's PLN 13,270m.
  • Free cash flow: the model is better by PLN 0.09bn (FY2028), 0.16bn (FY2029) and 0.20bn (FY2030). I reproduced the model's figure exactly as EBITDA less tax, less net financial items, less the change in working capital, less capital spending, less leases. Two things differ from the second set's own build:
  • The second set deducts PLN 0.5bn to 0.6bn a year of restructuring and reclamation cash and adds back about PLN 0.45bn of non-cash provision unwinding. The model has no line for either. Net, the second set is PLN 0.05bn to 0.15bn lower.
  • The model releases working capital when revenue falls (PLN 20m in FY2028 and PLN 121m in FY2029, at 6.0% of revenue). The second set did not count that release.
  • For FY2029 and FY2030 the second set's figure is the better guide, because the reclamation cash is real. The difference adds about PLN 0.45bn to net debt by 2030, against PLN 36bn. I leave the inputs alone.
  • Net debt: the model's year-end figures are about PLN 2bn above the second set's "company net debt" in every year (FY2026: PLN 9.0bn against 6.9bn). The model starts from FactSet's net debt, which includes leases, and rolls forward from the reported June 2026 balance. In the first half of 2026 FactSet's free cash flow (about plus PLN 4.6bn) exceeded the fall in FactSet's net debt (PLN 2.6bn) by about PLN 2bn. On my reading, that PLN 2bn is the model's stated reconciliation difference of PLN 2.2bn: cash that left through lines the model does not carry (new leases, acquisitions, other financing items). It is a difference of definition, not of view. Use the second set's economic net debt for rating questions and the bank definition for the 3.5-times ceiling (D-01, point 3).

Cautions on the AlphaSense reports

  • The coal capacity figures attributed to Fitch belong to Tauron. Two reports disagree on whose they are. The only source cited for them is Fitch's rating action on Tauron of 28 September 2026. I treat them as Tauron's (D-01, point 2).
  • The narrative report mixes in material on Portland General Electric (a "2027 General Rate Case", Jefferies on "POR"). Ignore those passages. It also says the 22% tax rate is unconfirmed; three other reports document the Senate's vote of 6 November 2026.
  • The "other side of the story" paragraphs are assertions, not evidence. I have used only the dated, sourced findings.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of PGE-PL with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-13 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: PGE Polska Grupa Energetyczna S.A. (PGE-PL, Warsaw; not Portland General Electric or PG&E): Poland's largest state-controlled integrated power group (State Treasury 60.86%); segments: Distribution (regulated grid), District Heating, Coal Energy (lignite and hard-coal generation plus own lignite mines), Gas-fired Generation, Renewables (onshore, solar, storage, and offshore wind including the 50% Baltica 2 joint operation with Ørsted), Supply (retail), and Railway Energy Services.

Request: OWN-1

Question: For PGE Polska Grupa Energetyczna (PGE-PL, Warsaw; not Portland General Electric or PG&E), what has been published since 11 November 2026 on the events that decide its 2027 earnings, and how have brokers responded? (1) What did PGE's third-quarter 2026 preliminary figures and report (due 24 November 2026) show, by segment, for recurring EBITDA (Distribution, District Heating, Coal Energy, Gas-fired Generation, Renewables, Supply, Railway Energy Services), for capital spending in the first nine months of 2026, for net debt and economic net debt, and for the onerous-contract provisions at PGE Obrót and PGE GiEK (PLN 218m and PLN 697m at 30 June 2026, Q2 2026 interim report)? How did each segment compare with brokers' estimates, and what did management say on the call about the outlook for each segment in 2027 (as it did for 2026 with the third-quarter 2025 results on 26 November 2025)? (2) Did President Karol Nawrocki sign or veto the act raising corporate income tax from 19% to 22% from 1 January 2027 (passed by the Senate on 6 November 2026)? What is the status and scope of draft bill UD459 (temporary higher corporate tax for large fuel and energy companies), and does PGE's own lignite mining fall within "fossil fuel extraction" under it? (3) What did the Energy Regulatory Office approve for 2027 for PGE Dystrybucja (the distribution tariff, the allowed return including the reinvestment premium, against the fixed base of 7.478% for 2023-2028 and the reductions seen for 2026 at Enea and Tauron) and for PGE Obrót (the household G tariff, against PLN 495.16/MWh for 2026)? What onerous-contract provision did PGE, Tauron, Enea and Energa announce after the decision? (4) What household electricity price cap or shield has the government adopted for 2027, how are suppliers compensated, and how is it funded: from the state budget, or from a levy, revenue cap or Price Difference Fund-type contribution on power generators? (5) What were the results of the December 2026 capacity market main auction for PGE, and is there any new statement from the Polish government, the grid operator PSE or the European Commission on support for coal units after 2028 (strategic reserve, capacity-market extension, statutory compensation)? (6) Since 21 October 2026, how has each covering broker (Trigon, Erste, PKO BP, BOS, Wood & Company, Noble, Bernstein, Santander, Pekao, IPOPEMA, Citigroup, Morgan Stanley) changed its FY2026-FY2030 EBITDA, EPS, depreciation, net financial result, tax rate, capital spending, free cash flow and dividend forecasts, its rating and its target price for PGE, and which of them now publish FY2029 and FY2030 forecasts and with what Coal Energy EBITDA? State each broker's definition of EBITDA and of adjusted EPS where the document gives it. Cover the same events for TAURON Polska Energia and Enea for read-across. Date every item, credit a view to a house only when citing that house's own document, separate what the evidence shows from inference, and say plainly what has not yet been published.

Request: OWN-2

Question: For PGE Polska Grupa Energetyczna (PGE-PL, Warsaw; not Portland General Electric or PG&E), who has been buying and selling the shares during the 2026 rally, and what are investors, as distinct from sell-side analysts, saying about the stock? PGE's shares rose from a 52-week low of PLN 8.21 to a high of PLN 17.55 in 2026 (Warsaw Stock Exchange prices to 6 November 2026), while FactSet showed the share of brokers with a buy rating at 29% and the mean target price at PLN 15.56 (9 November 2026). (1) What do investors say about PGE on X (Twitter), on Polish investor forums and portals (for example Bankier.pl, StockWatch.pl, Stooq, Parkiet, Puls Biznesu), in fund managers' letters and interviews, and in the Polish financial press: which themes are discussed most (the dividend from FY2026 earnings, the capacity contracts to 2028, the 2026 energy-price shock, Baltica 2, the low P/E, the coal problem after 2028, the 2027 election, taxes and levies), since when, and with what view; and which themes are not discussed at all? Quote and date representative items and name the commentators. (2) What do shareholder registers, fund disclosures and exchange data show about changes in ownership of PGE in 2026: Polish pension funds (OFE) and investment funds (TFI), foreign institutions, retail investors, and passive funds; PGE's weight in WIG20, MSCI Poland and other indices and any index changes in 2026 or scheduled for 2027; short interest and securities lending; and daily turnover around the main events (the Q2 2026 results on 16 September 2026, the 2027 supplementary capacity auction result, the Strategy 2035 update on 19-20 October 2026)? Did Orlen's increase of its stake in Energa to 95.24% (1 July 2026) and its buyout offer move money into PGE? (3) How did PGE's share price and valuation behave in its earlier rallies and the periods that followed them, for example 2021 (record EBITDA and the coal carve-out plan), 2023-2024 (the NABE plan, then the fall in December 2024 when the plan was put in doubt) and 2012-2016: how long did each rally last, what ended it, and how far did the shares fall afterwards? Do the same for TAURON Polska Energia and Enea, and say what investors in Polish state-controlled utilities are and are not focused on now. State near the top how many investor and ownership sources were found and used, date every item, separate evidence from inference, and say where the evidence is thin.

Request: OWN-3

Question: For PGE Polska Grupa Energetyczna (PGE-PL, Warsaw; not Portland General Electric or PG&E), what multiple of earnings have investors paid for PGE and for comparable utilities at each stage of an earnings cycle, and what happened to the multiple and the share price as a dated fall in earnings approached? PGE's coal capacity-market contracts end in December 2028 under the EU derogation, and management's Strategy 2035 update (20 October 2026) plans Coal Energy EBITDA of minus PLN 3.3bn in 2030; FactSet showed PGE's 24-month-forward P/E at 6.4x on 30 October 2026, against a five-year range of 3.3x to 11.2x and a median of 5.3x. (1) For PGE itself over the last ten to fifteen years: at what forward P/E and EV/EBITDA did the shares trade at each peak and trough of earnings and of the share price, what were consensus estimates doing at the time (rising, flat, falling), and what explained the highest multiples (for example around 11x) and the lowest (around 3x)? (2) Precedents of a known, dated loss of support or earnings: TAURON Polska Energia and Enea before and after their five-year coal capacity contracts ended in 2025; CEZ before, during and after the 60% windfall tax of 2023-2025 and through its accelerated coal depreciation; RWE and Uniper through their coal phase-out and compensation decisions; Public Power Corporation through its lignite exit by end-2026; Verbund and EVN under the Austrian energy-crisis levy extended to 2030; Fortum and Endesa where relevant. For each: when did sell-side estimates for the affected year first show the fall, how far ahead of the date did the share price and the forward multiple start to reflect it, did the multiple rise as earnings fell or fall with them, and what was the total shareholder return from 24 months before the date to 12 months after it? (3) Where a government later granted replacement support or compensation (RWE and LEAG lignite compensation; strategic reserves approved by the European Commission for Estonia in October 2025 and Sweden in July 2025; any Polish, Czech, German or Greek case), how did the shares and the multiple react on announcement and over the following year, and how much of the support had been anticipated in broker estimates and target prices beforehand? (4) What multiples do brokers currently apply to each of PGE's segments in sum-of-the-parts valuations (Distribution, District Heating, Renewables and offshore, Gas-fired Generation, Railway Energy Services, Supply, Coal Energy and its liabilities), what value per share does each segment contribute, and how have those valuations and the implied group multiple changed since the Strategy 2035 update? Cover every named company, date every figure, credit a valuation to a house only when citing that house's own document, separate evidence from inference, and conclude with the range of multiples the precedents support for a utility in each stage of such a cycle and the indicators that marked the turn from one stage to the next.
